\fupstart{25}{Part III: Report 25}
\part{Entire native fibers: Pell parametrization and height laws}\label{fup:part:nf}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~07 of batch~82: \emph{Research report 25}, dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Entire_Native_Witness_Fiber_Package.zip}; pin \texttt{ad634b2d1}. It is the sequel of Report~22. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 25.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
For fixed valid padded AND ports and prescribed dyadic scale, we classify every positive witness of the retained twenty-two-coordinate native arithmetic component. Seventeen coordinates are forced. The remaining five are in bijection with two integer indices: $m=M\ell$, $\ell\ge1$, and $n=p$ or $n=4mk\pm p$, $k\ge1$, where $M=pc/\gcd(c,A^2-1)$ and $c=\psi_A(p)$. The maximum supplied-coordinate height is exactly the last Pell coordinate, with no native fixed-coordinate plateau. This yields an exact finite floor formula and the unsmoothed two-term law
\[
 N(H)=\kappa\log H+
 \frac{\zeta(1/2)}{M\sqrt{\log\alpha}}\sqrt{\log H}
 +O\bigl((\log H)^{1/3}\bigr).
\]
The coefficient $\kappa$ includes an exceptional baseline family and an exact convergent series. We prove a corresponding logarithmic ranked-height expansion, allowing ties. Native soundness and positive completeness are explicitly inherited from pinned source proofs; the Pell rank, congruence and analytic arguments are included with their attribution. A companion theorem counts the sum of all coordinate bit lengths. A portable standard-library verification packet accompanies the article.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
The classification is conditional on the pinned native soundness and positive-completeness theorems, as the abstract and Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:main} say. The programme's review \rw{review_incoming_negative_index_fibers.md} (commit \texttt{0055e1c4d}) finds the classification consistent and does not certify the second-term or total-bit-length theorems. In this Part $\alpha=A+\sqrt\Delta$ and $\lambda=\log\alpha$; Report~34 in Part~\ref{fup:part:ir} uses the two letters the other way round (Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:notation}). The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{07-native-fiber-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{The object being counted}
The native component is a fixed integer arithmetic circuit, not an unspecified Diophantine representation. Fix relation arguments
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:domain}
 P=2^b\ (b\in\Z_{\ge0}),\qquad
 h_0=\widehat H-1,\quad v_0=\widehat M-1,\quad z_0=\widehat Z-1,
 \qquad 0\le h_0,v_0<P,\quad z_0=h_0\AND v_0.
\end{equation}
All four relation arguments are positive integers. Its computed padded ports are
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:ports}
 q=16P,\qquad A_{\mathrm{port}}=16h_0+12,\qquad
 B_{\mathrm{port}}=16v_0+10,\qquad F_3=16z_0+8.
\end{equation}
The twenty-two \emph{supplied} native coordinates, all required to lie in $\Np$, are
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:coords}
\begin{gathered}
 F_0,F_1,F_2,a,c,d,f,h,i,j,k,o,r,s,w,\\
 \tau,\eta,\zeta,\mathit{ga},y,\mathit{odd\_half},\mathit{bound\_beta}.
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Here $y$ denotes the literal coordinate \file{y_aux}. The symbol $\zeta$ in this coordinate list is unrelated to the Riemann zeta function. Computed quantities, including $q,F_3,X,Y$, are not additional supplied coordinates. Positivity is imposed on the supplied coordinates; intermediate subtraction registers follow the literal circuit.

We write $\mathcal F$ for the full positive fiber at the fixed arguments~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:domain}. The height and its ordinary counting function are
\[
 \operatorname{ht}(w)=\max\{\text{the twenty-two coordinates of }w\},
 \qquad N(H)=\#\{w\in\mathcal F:\operatorname{ht}(w)\le H\}.
\]
Different tuples count separately, even if their heights coincide. All logarithms are natural, and all asymptotic constants may depend on the fixed ports and scale.

For an integer $B\ge2$, define the Pell sequences by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:pell}
 \chi_B(t)+\psi_B(t)\sqrt{B^2-1}
 =\bigl(B+\sqrt{B^2-1}\bigr)^t\qquad(t\in\Z).
\end{equation}
The polynomial and addition identities used below follow either by multiplying these expressions or by the recurrence $u_{t+2}=2Bu_{t+1}-u_t$. In particular $\chi_B(-t)=\chi_B(t)$ and $\psi_B(-t)=-\psi_B(t)$.

\subsection{Precisely stated inherited mathematics}
All upstream native sources are pinned to the same repository commit
\begin{center}\small\texttt{ad634b2d10ad666260f9fdff04ec94b75169ee4b}.\end{center}
The selector soundness theorem~\cite{fup:nf:selector}, its prescribed-scale interface~\cite{fup:nf:interface}, the relaxed-norm proof~\cite{fup:nf:relaxed} and fixed-minus proof~\cite{fup:nf:parity} are supplied as inert text in the accompanying packet. We use these specific conclusions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Every positive native witness has the forced main indices and exponent/rounding values displayed in Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:forced}. These are forward soundness conclusions about \emph{every} witness, established before any preferred converse witness is selected.
\item Every valid tuple~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:domain} admits a strictly positive native extension at its prescribed scale. Thus the uniquely forced quotients and differences below are positive integers.
\item The native bootstrap, at the base $A=a+2$, establishes $c>A(A^2-1)^2$ before invoking the relaxed norm. The source obtains the main index $p=2r+1$, and $p\equiv3\pmod4$.
\end{enumerate}
The new classification is conditional on these proved native representation results. It does not purport to rebuild the entire representation theorem from elementary arithmetic. We give the relevant relaxed-norm rank proof and signed congruence argument in full because their exact hypotheses are essential to counting the whole fiber.

The predecessor Report 22 established uniqueness of the canonical outer history and a bijection from its complete positive witness fiber to this native fiber, as well as native infinitude. Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:transport} records the resulting transport of the present classification. The original Report 22 and its frozen circuits are unchanged.
\begin{writenote}
Report~22 is archive \rw{Canonical_Histories_and_Infinite_Fibers_Package.zip} of batch~82 (cluster~M2), placed by commit \texttt{7d2b1b245} as source~23 of \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, in its Part~VIII. Its theorem ``Fixed-scale native infinitude'' (three native coordinates vary while nineteen stay fixed) is a corollary of Theorem~\ref{fup:nf:thm:class}, which fixes exactly seventeen and parametrizes the other five; Report~22's elementary argument is a second route and is printed with Report~22.
\end{writenote}

\section{The complete result}\label{fup:nf:sec:main}
Using the forced values in Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:forced}, set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:fixedparameters}
 A=a+2,\quad \Delta=A^2-1,\quad p=2r+1,\quad c=\psi_A(p),\quad
 \alpha=A+\sqrt\Delta,\quad \lambda=\log\alpha,
\end{equation}
\[
 g=\gcd(c,\Delta)=\gcd(p,\Delta),\qquad M=\frac{pc}{g}.
\]
The native hypotheses give $A\ge3$, $p\ge3$, $p\equiv3\pmod4$, and $M\ge c>2p$.

\begin{theorem}[Entire positive native fiber]\label{fup:nf:thm:class}
Exactly seventeen coordinates of~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:coords} are fixed throughout $\mathcal F$. The other five, $f,i,j,o,y$, are parametrized without repetition by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:indices}
 m=M\ell\quad(\ell\ge1),\qquad
 n=p\quad\text{or}\quad n=4mk-p,\ 4mk+p\quad(k\ge1).
\end{equation}
For each such pair define
\begin{align}\label{fup:nf:eq:reconstruct}
 f_m&=\chi_A(m),& R_m&=\Delta\psi_A(m),& i_m&=R_m/c^2,\notag\\
 U_{m,n}&=\chi_{R_m}(n)/R_m,& j_{m,n}&=(U_{m,n}+p)/c,\\
 o_{m,n}&=(U_{m,n}+c)/f_m,& y_{m,n}&=\psi_{R_m}(n).\notag
\end{align}
All five reconstructed coordinates are positive integers. Adjoining the seventeen forced coordinates gives every member of $\mathcal F$, and
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:height}
 \operatorname{ht}(m,n)=y_{m,n}=\psi_{R_m}(n).
\end{equation}
In fact all seventeen fixed supplied coordinates are strictly smaller than $c^2\le R_m<y_{m,n}$.
\end{theorem}

Thus even the baseline $n=p$ is present for every $m=M\ell$, including $m=M$; the canonical converse choice $m=2cp$ is not a restriction on the answer.

Put
\[
 D_m=R_m^2-1,\qquad \varepsilon_m=R_m+\sqrt{D_m},\qquad
 \beta_m=\log\varepsilon_m,\qquad
 t_m(H)=\frac{\arsinh(H\sqrt{D_m})}{\beta_m}.
\]
For $H\ge1$, let $L_H$ be the largest $\ell\ge1$ with $\psi_{R_{M\ell}}(p)\le H$, or zero if no such $\ell$ exists.

\begin{theorem}[Finite count and two height laws]\label{fup:nf:thm:count}
The integer $L_H$ is finite and the exact count is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:count}
 N(H)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_H}\left(
 \floor{\frac{t_m(H)+4m-p}{4m}}+
 \floor{\frac{t_m(H)+p}{4m}}\right),\qquad m=M\ell.
\end{equation}
The same formula holds with $t_m(H)$ replaced by $\floor{t_m(H)}$. One may also replace the upper summation bound by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:explicitcut}
 K_0(H)=\floor{\frac{\log H}{M(p-1)\lambda}};
\end{equation}
any added summands are zero. For $H<1$, $N(H)=0$.

Define the positive constant
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:kappa}
 \kappa=\frac{1}{M(p-1)\lambda}
      +\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2M\ell\,\beta_{M\ell}},
 \qquad d_*^{\mathrm{count}}=\frac{\zeta(1/2)}{M\sqrt\lambda}<0.
\end{equation}
The series converges absolutely. Pointwise, including exact jump thresholds,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:asymptotic}
 N(H)=\kappa\log H+d_*^{\mathrm{count}}\sqrt{\log H}
       +O\bigl((\log H)^{1/3}\bigr).
\end{equation}
If $H_v$ is the height of the $v$-th tuple in nondecreasing height order, counting ties with multiplicity, then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:inverse}
 \log H_v=\frac v\kappa-
 \frac{\zeta(1/2)}{M\sqrt\lambda\,\kappa^{3/2}}\sqrt v
 +O(v^{1/3}).
\end{equation}
This is a logarithmic asymptotic, not a multiplicative equivalent for $H_v$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Seventeen forced coordinates and three varying comparisons}\label{fup:nf:sec:forced}
\subsection{Explicit formulas and universal forcing}
The padded ports, checksum and packing comparison first force
\begin{align}\label{fup:nf:eq:fields}
 F_1&=16(h_0-z_0)+4,&F_2&=16(v_0-z_0)+2,\notag\\
 F_0&=16(P-h_0-v_0+z_0)-15,&
 r&=F_0+qF_1+q^2F_2+q^3F_3.
\end{align}
Since $h_0+v_0-z_0=h_0\OR v_0<P$, $F_0\ge1$; the other fields are positive as well. The checksum gives $F_0+F_1+F_2+F_3+1=q$. Because $F_0$ is odd and $q$ is even, $r$ is odd, hence $p=2r+1\equiv3\pmod4$.

The forward selector theorem, Sections 2--3 of~\cite{fup:nf:selector}, gives
\begin{align}\label{fup:nf:eq:forcecore}
 X&=2^p,&
 Y&=\floor{\frac{(X+1)^{2r}}{X^r}}
   =\binom{2r}{r}+\sum_{v=1}^r\binom{2r}{r+v}X^v,\notag\\
 w&=X/q,&s&=Y/q,\notag\\
 a&=Y(X+1),& c&=\psi_A(p),\qquad d=\chi_A(p),\\
 E&=XY,& B&=2XY^2+1,\qquad k=\psi_B(r+1).\notag
\end{align}
The notation $B$ renames the first Pell base to distinguish it from the external scale $P$. The remaining forced coordinates are
\begin{align}\label{fup:nf:eq:forceslacks}
 h&=\frac{k-r-1}{E},&\eta&=c-Yk,&\zeta&=(Y+1)k-c,\notag\\
 \mathit{ga}&=\frac{d-X-ac}{4a+3},&
 \tau&=\frac{\chi_B(r+1)-1}{2},\\
 \mathit{odd\_half}&=\frac{s-1}{2},&
 \mathit{bound\_beta}&=X-r.\notag
\end{align}
In particular, the seventeen fixed names are
\[
 F_0,F_1,F_2,r,w,s,a,c,d,k,h,\eta,\zeta,\mathit{ga},
 \tau,\mathit{odd\_half},\mathit{bound\_beta}.
\]
This enumeration includes no computed register.

Here universal soundness is doing real work: merely writing one canonical witness would not prove these values unique. The source first recovers the indices $p=2r+1$ and $r+1$, and then the exponent $X$ and rounded value $Y$, for every positive witness. Subsequent divisions have positive denominators. The first norm determines the positive square root
\[
 (2\tau+1)^2-(B^2-1)k^2=1,
 \qquad 2\tau+1=\chi_B(r+1),
\]
so it forces $\tau$ as well. Finally the source's positive converse at the same valid ports proves that the unique values in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:fields}--\eqref{fup:nf:eq:forceslacks} are indeed positive integers. This invocation does not impose its auxiliary-index choice on other witnesses.

\subsection{The complete literal comparison list}
Let $U=jc-p$ and $R=ic^2$. The following are all sixteen comparisons of the native block, written as equalities of their actual polynomials. The right column gives incidence in the potentially varying set $\{f,i,j,o,y\}$.
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}r p{0.68\linewidth} l@{}}\toprule
No.&Comparison&Incidence\\\midrule
1&$r=F_0+qF_1+q^2F_2+q^3F_3$&none\\
2&$F_0+F_1+F_2+F_3+1=q$&none\\
3&$s=2\mathit{odd\_half}+1$&none\\
4&$r+\mathit{bound\_beta}=X$&none\\
5&$(E^2+X)(Yk)^2=\tau(\tau+1)$&none\\
6&$c=Yk+\eta$&none\\
7&$k=\eta+\zeta$&none\\
8&$k=r+1+hE$&none\\
9&$a=Y(X+1)$&none\\
10&$d=X+ac+\mathit{ga}(4a+3)$&none\\
11&$d^2=1+\Delta c^2$&none\\
12&$(ic^2)^2=\Delta(f^2-1)$&$f,i$\\
13&$(ic^2)^2(U^2-y^2)=1-y^2$&$i,j,y$\\
14&$U=of-c$&$f,j,o$\\
15&$F_1+F_3=16h_0+12$&none\\
16&$F_2+F_3=16v_0+10$&none\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The parentheses in comparison 12 are essential: its right side is $\Delta(f^2-1)$, not $\Delta f^2-1$. It is the relaxed main auxiliary norm; comparison 11 is the fixed main Pell norm.

The literal-DAG audit independently expands all sixteen residuals, propagates transitive dependencies, checks the twenty-two supplied names, and checks the acyclic sixty-four-gate source on four retained fixtures. Consequently exactly thirteen comparisons are unaffected when the five coordinates change. All recomputed registers are determined by the unchanged acyclic DAG. Once the forced values are installed, the entire positive fiber is therefore exactly the set of positive solutions of
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:three}
 R^2=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 (RU)^2-(R^2-1)y^2=1,\qquad U=jc-p=of-c,
 \qquad R=ic^2.
\end{equation}
The general valid-port theorem comes from~\cite{fup:nf:interface}; it is not inferred by extrapolating finite fixture tests.

\section{The exact main auxiliary progression}\label{fup:nf:sec:mainindex}
The relaxed norm alone can have unwanted rational Pell behavior. The large native bound $c>A\Delta^2$ is what excludes it. The following proof is the rank argument of~\cite{fup:nf:relaxed}, restated at $A=a+2$; after its bootstrap the argument only needs the hypotheses displayed here.

\begin{lemma}[Inherited relaxed-norm rank lemma]\label{fup:nf:lem:rank}
Suppose $A\ge2$, $\Delta=A^2-1$, $c=\psi_A(p)>A\Delta^2$, and positive integers $R,f$ satisfy $c^2\mid R$ and $R^2=\Delta(f^2-1)$. Then for a unique $m\in\Np$,
\[
 p\mid m,\qquad f=\chi_A(m),\qquad R=\Delta\psi_A(m).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $\Delta=d_0s_0^2$ with $d_0>1$ squarefree. Choose the least positive norm-one unit with integer coefficients in $\Z[\sqrt{d_0}]$, say $F+y_0\sqrt{d_0}$. It exists because $A+s_0\sqrt{d_0}$ is one such unit. Every positive integer-coefficient norm-one unit is its power: division by the appropriate power leaves an integer-coefficient unit in $[1,F+y_0\sqrt{d_0})$, contradicting minimality unless it equals one. Hence for some $e\ge1$,
\[
 A=\chi_F(e),\quad S=\psi_F(e),\quad s_0=y_0S,
 \quad\Delta=(F^2-1)S^2,\quad\psi_F(ep)=Sc.
\]
The relaxed norm gives $s_0\mid R$. Then $(R/s_0)^2=d_0(f^2-1)$ and squarefreeness gives $d_0\mid R/s_0$. Thus $f+R/(d_0s_0)\sqrt{d_0}$ is an integer-coefficient norm-one unit, so for some $v>0$,
\[
 f=\chi_F(v),\qquad R=T_0\psi_F(v),\qquad
 T_0=(F^2-1)S=\Delta/S.
\]
The sequence $\psi_F$ is strongly divisible:
\[
 \gcd(\psi_F(u),\psi_F(v))=\psi_F(\gcd(u,v)).
\]
Indeed the norm gives $\gcd(\chi_F(t),\psi_F(t))=1$, and the addition identity reduces the gcd by the Euclidean algorithm. Put $b_0=\gcd(v,ep)$ and $h_1=\psi_F(b_0)$. Since $c^2\mid T_0\psi_F(v)$,
\[
 c\le\gcd(c^2,T_0\psi_F(ep))
    =c\gcd(c,\Delta)\le T_0h_1,
 \qquad h_1\ge c/\Delta.
\]
If $b_0<ep$, then $2b_0\le ep$, and doubling yields
\[
 2h_1^2<\psi_F(2b_0)\le\psi_F(ep)=Sc<Ac.
\]
Here $S<A$, as follows from $\Delta=(F^2-1)S^2<A^2$ and $F\ge2$. But $h_1^2\ge c^2/\Delta^2>Ac$, a contradiction. Therefore $ep\mid v$. Write $v=em$; Pell composition gives the stated formulas and $p\mid m$. Strict increase of $\chi_A$ proves uniqueness.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{fup:nf:prop:m}
For the native fixed parameters, the possible main auxiliary indices are exactly $m=M\ell$, $\ell\ge1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The lemma gives $m=pk$ and $R=\Delta\psi_A(pk)$. Expanding $(d+c\sqrt\Delta)^k$ and dividing its square-root coefficient by $c$ gives
\[
 \frac{\psi_A(pk)}c\equiv k d^{k-1}\pmod c.
\]
All terms after the linear term contain $c^2$. Since $d^2-\Delta c^2=1$, $\gcd(d,c)=1$. Therefore
\begin{align*}
 c^2\mid\Delta\psi_A(pk)
 &\iff c\mid\Delta k d^{k-1}
 \iff c\mid\Delta k\\
 &\iff (c/g)\mid k
 \iff M\mid m,
 \qquad g=\gcd(c,\Delta).
\end{align*}
This is an equivalence, not only a necessary divisibility condition. Also the binomial expansion modulo $\Delta$ gives
\[
 c\equiv pA^{p-1}\pmod\Delta,
 \qquad\gcd(A,\Delta)=1,
\]
so $g=\gcd(p,\Delta)$ and $g\mid p$. Hence $M$ is a multiple of $c$ and $m\ge M\ge c>2p$. Conversely every $m=M\ell$ has $p\mid m$ and $c^2\mid R_m$, so $i_m=R_m/c^2$ is a positive integer and the first equation of~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:three} holds. It remains to solve the other two equations; this is done next.
\end{proof}
No evenness condition on $m$ or oddness condition on $f_m$ has been imposed. Those features of some canonical constructions are not requirements of the whole fiber.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{A domain distinction} (from the programme's review \rw{review_incoming_negative_index_fibers.md}, commit \texttt{0055e1c4d}, which finds the classification consistent). The progression $m=M\ell$, $M=pc/g$, is for the ordinary source, where the coefficient $\Delta$ multiplies $\psi_A(m)$. It must not be copied to the tree's normalized strong equation, where $c^2\mid\psi_A(m)$ forces $m$ to be a multiple of $pc$. The review's auxiliary-only example $A=2$, $p=3$, $c=\psi_2(3)=15$ has $g=3$, $M=15$, against $45$ in the normalized case.
\end{writenote}

\section{The two normalized Pell progressions}\label{fup:nf:sec:normalized}
Fix any $m=M\ell$. Then $c\mid m$, $R=R_m$ is a positive multiple of $c^2$, and $f=f_m>2c$. The latter follows from $m>2p$ and
$\chi_A(2p)=1+2\Delta c^2>2c$.

\subsection{Generation and the odd-index congruences}
For $B\ge2$, the least positive integer-coefficient norm-one unit in $\Z[\sqrt{B^2-1}]$ is $B+\sqrt{B^2-1}$: a positive integer second coordinate is at least one, making the first coordinate at least $B$. The minimal-unit argument from Lemma~\ref{fup:nf:lem:rank} therefore proves that every positive solution is $(\chi_B(n),\psi_B(n))$ for a unique $n\ge1$. This concerns the integer-coefficient group, not the full ring of integers of the quadratic field; no unjustified fundamental-unit claim is needed.

For a native witness, $U=jc-p\ge c-p>0$, so the second equation in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:three} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:normalizedpell}
 RU=\chi_R(n),\qquad y=\psi_R(n),\qquad n\ge1.
\end{equation}
Modulo $R$, the recurrence gives
$\chi_R(2h)\equiv(-1)^h$ and $\chi_R(2h+1)\equiv0$.
Since $R>1$ and $R\mid\chi_R(n)$, $n$ must be odd.

For $n=2h+1$, write $\chi_X(2h+1)=XQ_h(X^2)$, where
\[
 Q_0(t)=1,\quad Q_1(t)=4t-3,\quad
 Q_{h+2}(t)=(4t-2)Q_{h+1}(t)-Q_h(t).
\]
Induction gives
\[
 Q_h(0)=(-1)^h(2h+1),\qquad
 Q_h(1-A^2)=(-1)^h\psi_A(2h+1).
\]
Since $c\mid R$ and $R^2=\Delta(f^2-1)\equiv1-A^2\pmod f$, these identities imply
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:congruences}
 U\equiv(-1)^h n\pmod c,\qquad
 U\equiv(-1)^h\psi_A(n)\pmod f.
\end{equation}
The reconstruction of $j$ and $o$ requires exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:minuses}
 U\equiv-p\pmod c,\qquad U\equiv-c\pmod f.
\end{equation}
These polynomial identities and the sign analysis below are the fixed-minus argument in~\cite{fup:nf:parity}, with its normalized index renamed $n$.

\subsection{Step down and necessity}
\begin{lemma}[Plus-sign step down]\label{fup:nf:lem:step}
If $B\ge2$, $0<k_0\le m$ and
$\chi_B(v)\equiv\chi_B(k_0)\pmod{\chi_B(m)}$, then
$v\equiv\pm k_0\pmod{4m}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The identities give $\chi_B(v+2m)\equiv-\chi_B(v)$ modulo $\chi_B(m)$ and $\chi_B(-v)=\chi_B(v)$. Thus reduce to a representative $\pm\chi_B(l)$, $0\le l\le m$. For $k_0<m$ and $l<m$,
\[
 \chi_B(l)+\chi_B(k_0)\le2\chi_B(m-1)<\chi_B(m).
\]
Strict monotonicity therefore excludes the negative representative and forces $l=k_0$ for the positive one. The case $l=m$ is zero modulo the modulus and cannot occur when $k_0<m$. When $k_0=m$, only $l=m$ is zero. Translating these representatives gives exactly the two residues asserted.
\end{proof}

Squaring the second congruence in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:congruences}--\eqref{fup:nf:eq:minuses} and using $\chi_A(2t)=1+2\Delta\psi_A(t)^2$ yields
\[
 \chi_A(2n)\equiv\chi_A(2p)\pmod f.
\]
Since $0<2p<m$, Lemma~\ref{fup:nf:lem:step} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:halfresidue}
 n=e p+2mt,\qquad e\in\{1,-1\},\quad t\in\Z.
\end{equation}
This division by two is a division of an integer equality after obtaining the residue modulo $4m$; it is not cancellation of a possibly nonunit in a residue ring.

The addition identities give
\[
 \psi_A(n)\equiv e(-1)^t c\pmod f,
 \qquad n\equiv e p\pmod c.
\]
With $p=2r+1$ and $h=(n-1)/2$, both signs satisfy
$(-1)^he=(-1)^{r+mt}$. The two original, unsquared minus congruences become
\[
 (-1)^{r+mt}p\equiv-p\pmod c,
 \qquad(-1)^{r+(m+1)t}c\equiv-c\pmod f.
\]
Because $c>2p$ and $f>2c$, the respective exponents must both be odd. Their difference is $t$, which is therefore even. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:nresidue}
 n\equiv p\ \text{or}\ -p\pmod{4m}.
\end{equation}
The same argument recovers the necessary oddness of $r$, consistent with the padded fields.

\subsection{Converse and full-fiber bijection}
Conversely let $n>0$ have either residue in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:nresidue}. It is odd, so $U=\chi_R(n)/R$ is a positive integer. Write $n=e p+4mk$. The same identities give
\[
 \psi_A(n)\equiv e c\pmod f,\quad n\equiv e p\pmod c,
 \quad (-1)^{(n-1)/2}e=(-1)^r=-1.
\]
Thus both congruences~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:minuses} hold. Hence $j=(U+p)/c$ and $o=(U+c)/f$ are positive integers, and~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:normalizedpell} supplies the norm. All three varying comparisons hold, while the other thirteen keep their zero values. This proves positive completeness for every allowed pair in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:indices}.

The representatives $p$ and $4m-p$ are distinct and lie in $(0,4m)$ because $0<p<2m$. Their increasing merge is
\[
 p,\ 4m-p,\ 4m+p,\ 8m-p,\ 8m+p,\ldots.
\]
The zero-index Pell solution is excluded by $y>0$; negative indices have negative $y$, and a negative first coordinate contradicts $U>0$. Strict increase of $\chi_A(m)$ recovers $m$ from $f$, and strict increase of $\psi_{R_m}(n)$ then recovers $n$ from $y$. There is no double counting. Moreover $f,i$ vary as $m$ increases, and $j,o,y$ vary within each fixed-$m$ progression. This proves the exact seventeen/five partition and the bijection of Theorem~\ref{fup:nf:thm:class}.

\section{The native maximum is always the last Pell coordinate}\label{fup:nf:sec:height}
First control the fixed coordinates. The native bounds give
\[
 q<r<X<a<A<c.
\]
The formulas for the fields, $w,s,a,\mathit{odd\_half},\mathit{bound\_beta}$ therefore make each less than $c$. The relations give $k<c/Y<c$, $h<k$, $\eta<c$, and $\zeta<k$. Also
\[
 \frac dc=\sqrt{\Delta+c^{-2}}<A,
 \qquad d<Ac<c^2,
 \qquad \mathit{ga}<\frac d{4a+3}<c,
\]
because $4a+3>A$. The coordinate $c$ itself is below $c^2$.

For the remaining coordinate $\tau$, note $E=XY<a$ and $X<2E+1$. Thus
\[
 \tau^2<\tau(\tau+1)=(E^2+X)(Yk)^2<(E+1)^2(Yk)^2,
\]
whence $\tau<(E+1)Yk<(E+1)c<Ac<c^2$. This accounts for all seventeen fixed supplied coordinates.

For the five varying ones, the relaxed norm and $\Delta\ge3,f\ge2$ give
\[
 R^2=\Delta(f^2-1)>f^2,
 \qquad i=R/c^2\le R.
\]
Since $n\ge p\ge3$,
\[
 y=\psi_R(n)\ge\psi_R(3)=4R^2-1>R\ge c^2.
\]
The normalized norm yields
\[
 0<U\le y,\qquad
 U^2=y^2-\frac{y^2-1}{R^2}.
\]
Using $p<c$ and $c<f$,
\[
 U+p\le y+c-1\le cy,\qquad
 U+c\le y+f-1\le fy.
\]
Therefore $j,o\le y$. This proves~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:height} for the actual full native tuple.

An expression $\max(C,y)$ remains correct if one fixes other coordinates and calls their maximum $C$. Within the native twenty-two-coordinate fiber, however, the inequalities just proved always give $C<y$: there is no native initial plateau. A genuinely independent external fixed-coordinate maximum can create such a cutoff; Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:transport} keeps that case separate.

The smallest native height is attained uniquely at $(m,n)=(M,p)$. This follows from strict increase of $R_m$ in $m$ and of $\psi_R(n)$ in either argument in the present range. No assertion about componentwise minimality of the entire five-coordinate tuple is needed for this height conclusion.

\section{Exact finite counts and the fixed-slice phase}\label{fup:nf:sec:finite}
For fixed $m$,
\[
 \psi_{R_m}(n)=\frac{\sinh(n\beta_m)}{\sqrt{D_m}},
\]
so $y_{m,n}\le H$ if and only if $n\le t_m(H)$. Counting the two distinct residues yields the summand in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:count}. Since the smallest admissible index is $p$, only $\ell\le L_H$ contribute. The baseline height $\psi_{R_{M\ell}}(p)$ increases strictly to infinity, proving finiteness.

For $n\ge1$ the geometric-sum identity is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:geom}
 \psi_{R_m}(n)=\varepsilon_m^{n-1}
 \frac{1-\varepsilon_m^{-2n}}{1-\varepsilon_m^{-2}}\ge\varepsilon_m^{n-1}.
\end{equation}
Also $R_m>f_m=\cosh(m\lambda)$, so $\beta_m>m\lambda$. Thus a baseline point under height $H$ satisfies
$\log H\ge(p-1)m\lambda$, proving~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:explicitcut}.
The outer floors are unchanged on replacing $t_m$ by its integer part because their shifts and denominators are integers.

\subsection{The exact staircase inside one main-index slice}
For fixed $m$, put
\[
 \mathcal P_m(x)=1-\left\{\frac{x-p}{4m}\right\}
                  -\left\{\frac{x+p}{4m}\right\},
\]
where braces denote fractional part. The function is bounded and $4m$-periodic. For every $H\ge1$, the fixed-$m$ count is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:phase}
 N_m(H)=\frac{t_m(H)}{2m}+\mathcal P_m(t_m(H)).
\end{equation}
In particular $N_m(H)=\log H/(2m\beta_m)+O(1)$. The admissible density is $1/(2m)$ among all positive indices, or $1/m$ among odd indices.

The smooth inverse phase has the convergent expansion, for $H\sqrt{D_m}>1$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:phaseexpansion}
 t_m(H)=\frac{1}{\beta_m}\left[
 \log(2\sqrt{D_m}H)+\sum_{k\ge1}
 \frac{(-1)^{k-1}\binom{2k}{k}}{2k\,4^kD_m^kH^{2k}}
 \right].
\end{equation}
To verify it, expand the derivative of $\arsinh x-\log(2x)$ by the convergent binomial series for $x>1$, and integrate toward infinity. The first terms are $1/(4x^2)-3/(32x^4)+5/(96x^6)$. This smooth expansion must not be substituted approximately into a discontinuous floor with a claim of uniform correctness at jumps. The exact phase or exact integer comparisons are needed there.

The ranks within this slice have explicit indices
\[
 n_{2k+1}=4mk+p\ (k\ge0),\qquad
 n_{2k}=4mk-p\ (k\ge1),
\]
and exact heights $\psi_{R_m}(n_v)$. There are no initial repeated heights caused by fixed native coordinates. The least slice triple $(j,o,y)$ occurs at $n=p$; selecting it mathematically is not a new arithmetic gate or a finite-fold implementation.

\section{The leading coefficient and its exceptional baseline}\label{fup:nf:sec:leading}
Before obtaining the second term, it is useful to prove the leading law with a simpler uniform estimate. Write $T=\log H$ and separate
\[
 N(e^T)=B(T)+Q(T),
\]
where $B$ counts $n=p$ over varying $m$, and $Q$ counts $n=4mk\pm p$, $k\ge1$.

Since
\[
 R_m=\frac{\sqrt\Delta}{2}\alpha^m(1-\alpha^{-2m}),
\]
we have $\log R_m=m\lambda+O(1)$. For fixed $p$, $\psi_R(p)$ is a polynomial of degree $p-1$ with positive leading coefficient $2^{p-1}$; moreover $R^{p-1}\le\psi_R(p)\le(2R)^{p-1}$ for $R\ge2$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:baseline}
 \log\psi_{R_m}(p)=(p-1)m\lambda+O(1),\qquad
 B(T)=\frac{T}{M(p-1)\lambda}+O(1).
\end{equation}
The $p-1$ is essential: the varying Pell denominator must not be discarded.

The smallest nonbaseline index is $4m-p$. Since $m>2p$ and $p\ge3$,
$4m-p-1\ge3m$. Equation~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:geom} gives
\[
 \log\psi_{R_m}(4m-p)\ge3\lambda m^2.
\]
Thus nonbaseline slices are supported only at $m\le J(T)=\sqrt{T/(3\lambda)}$, whereas the baseline occupies $O(T)$ slices.

Uniformly in $m$ and $H\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:uniforminverse}
 0\le t_m(H)-\frac{\log H}{\beta_m}\le1.
\end{equation}
The lower bound follows from $\arsinh(H\sqrt{D_m})\ge\log H$. For the upper bound, $\arsinh(H\sqrt{D_m})-\log H$ decreases with $H\ge1$ and equals $\arsinh\sqrt{D_m}=\beta_m$ at $H=1$. The two floors now give $N_m(e^T)=T/(2m\beta_m)+O(1)$ with an absolute uniform error. Subtracting the baseline indicator preserves this bound. Summing only over $m\le J(T)$ therefore gives
\[
 Q(T)=T\sum_{M\ell\le J(T)}\frac{1}{2M\ell\beta_{M\ell}}+O(\sqrt T).
\]
Because $\beta_{M\ell}>M\ell\lambda$, the series tail after $K\ge1$ terms satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:tail}
 0<\sum_{\ell>K}\frac1{2M\ell\beta_{M\ell}}
 \le\frac1{2\lambda M^2K}.
\end{equation}
Extending the sum costs $O(\sqrt T)$ and proves the weaker law $N(e^T)=\kappa T+O(\sqrt T)$. Also
\[
 0<\kappa-\frac1{M(p-1)\lambda}\le\frac{\pi^2}{12\lambda M^2}.
\]
One cannot obtain the correct coefficient by simply summing fixed-slice leading terms: that misses the exceptional baseline. Nor can one sum a fixed-slice $O(1)$ error over the $O(T)$ baseline range.

\section{A pointwise second term without smoothing}\label{fup:nf:sec:second}
We now strengthen the remainder and identify its square-root term. The method is the classical hyperbola decomposition and Euler summation, applied to exact Pell heights. The exact sign-specific boundary below avoids any averaging of floor phases.

\subsection{Exact energy and uniform row and column estimates}
For this section set
\[
 q_0=M\lambda,\qquad b_0=\tfrac12\log\Delta,\qquad
 a_0=4\lambda M^2,\qquad
 \beta_\ell=\beta_{M\ell},\qquad g_\ell=4M\ell\beta_\ell.
\]
The elementary bounds are
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:betabounds}
 q_0\ell\le\beta_\ell\le q_0\ell+b_0.
\end{equation}
The lower bound was proved above. For the upper bound use
$R_{M\ell}=\sqrt\Delta\sinh(q_0\ell)$ and
$\cosh(q_0\ell+b_0)>\sqrt\Delta\sinh(q_0\ell)$.

For $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}$ define the nonbaseline energy
\[
 E_\sigma(\ell,k)=\log\psi_{R_{M\ell}}(4M\ell k+\sigma p),\qquad \ell,k\ge1.
\]
Taking logarithms in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:geom} gives the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:energy}
 E_\sigma(\ell,k)=g_\ell k+(\sigma p-1)\beta_\ell+\theta_{\ell,k,\sigma},
 \qquad 0\le\theta_{\ell,k,\sigma}\le d_0,
\end{equation}
where $d_0=-\log(1-e^{-2q_0})$. The bound holds because every normalized index here is at least $p$. In particular
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:quadratic}
 |E_\sigma(\ell,k)-a_0k\ell^2|\le Ck\ell,
 \qquad C=4Mb_0+(p+1)(q_0+b_0)+d_0.
\end{equation}
Also $g_\ell=a_0\ell^2+O(\ell)$, so
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:slopetail}
 S=\sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac1{g_\ell}<\infty,
 \qquad\sum_{\ell>L}\frac1{g_\ell}
       =\frac1{a_0L}+O(L^{-2}).
\end{equation}
The exact series is retained here. Replacing every $\beta_\ell$ by $q_0\ell$ in its definition would generally change the linear coefficient.

Let $q_\sigma(T,\ell)$ be the number of $k\ge1$ with $E_\sigma(\ell,k)\le T$. Exact inversion gives
\[
 q_\sigma(T,\ell)=\max\left(0,
 \floor{\frac{t_{M\ell}(e^T)-\sigma p}{4M\ell}}\right).
\]
By~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:uniforminverse}, uniformly for $T\ge0$ and $\ell\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:row}
 q_\sigma(T,\ell)=T/g_\ell+O(1).
\end{equation}
The positive-part clipping and floor each cost a bounded amount, since $T/g_\ell\ge0$ and $p/(4M\ell)$ is uniformly bounded.

Let $r_\sigma(T,k)$ be the number of $\ell\ge1$ with $E_\sigma(\ell,k)\le T$, and put $x=\sqrt{T/(a_0k)}$ and $D_0=C/a_0$. From~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:quadratic},
\[
 \ell^2-D_0\ell\le E_\sigma(\ell,k)/(a_0k)
                    \le\ell^2+D_0\ell.
\]
Every integer $\ell\le x-D_0$ is admitted, and every admitted $\ell$ satisfies $\ell\le x+D_0$. Including endpoint rounding and the possible empty range proves the uniform estimate
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:column}
 r_\sigma(T,k)=\sqrt{T/(a_0k)}+O(1).
\end{equation}
This estimates the count directly. It never assumes that a floor remains unchanged after replacing its argument by an asymptotic approximation.

The energy is strictly increasing in each positive integer variable. For fixed $k$, both $R_{M\ell}$ and $4M\ell k+\sigma p>1$ increase with $\ell$; $\psi_R(n)$ increases with $R$ and $n$ in this range. Finiteness follows, for example, from
\[
 E_\sigma(\ell,k)\ge(4M\ell k-p-1)M\ell\lambda
                      \ge3\lambda M^2k\ell^2.
\]

\subsection{An exact boundary rectangle}
For sufficiently large $T$ set
\[
 L=\floor{T^{1/3}},\qquad K_\sigma=q_\sigma(T,L).
\]
Equations~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:row} and~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:betabounds} give
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:K}
 K_\sigma=\frac{T}{a_0L^2}+O(T/L^3+1)
          =\frac{T}{a_0L^2}+O(1)\asymp T^{1/3}.
\end{equation}
Let $Q_\sigma(T)$ count all points of sign $\sigma$. Monotonicity gives the exact hyperbola identity
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:hyperbola}
 Q_\sigma(T)=\sum_{\ell\le L}q_\sigma(T,\ell)
          +\sum_{k\le K_\sigma}r_\sigma(T,k)-LK_\sigma.
\end{equation}
Every point in $1\le\ell\le L$, $1\le k\le K_\sigma$ is admitted because $E_\sigma(L,K_\sigma)\le T$. No point with both $\ell>L$ and $k>K_\sigma$ is admitted, since already $E_\sigma(L,K_\sigma+1)>T$. The two strips therefore cover the region and overlap in exactly that rectangle. This argument remains true when $T$ is exactly an energy, and uses a separate exact cutoff for each sign.

Put $u=\sqrt{T/a_0}$. Substituting~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:slopetail}, \eqref{fup:nf:eq:row}, and~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:column} into~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:hyperbola} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:beforeeuler}
 Q_\sigma(T)=ST-\frac{T}{a_0L}
 +u\sum_{k\le K_\sigma}k^{-1/2}-LK_\sigma
 +O(L+T/L^2+K_\sigma).
\end{equation}
The classical Euler-summation formula is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:euler}
 \sum_{k=1}^K k^{-1/2}=2\sqrt K+\zeta(1/2)+O(K^{-1/2}).
\end{equation}
For completeness, the identity recorded in DLMF 25.2.8~\cite{fup:nf:dlmf} at $s=1/2$ is
\[
 \zeta(1/2)=\sum_{k=1}^K k^{-1/2}-2\sqrt K
 -\frac12\int_K^\infty\{x\}x^{-3/2}\,dx.
\]
The integral contribution has absolute value at most $K^{-1/2}$, proving~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:euler}. The alternating-series identity DLMF 25.2.3 gives $\zeta(1/2)<0$ because its numerator is positive and its factor $1-\sqrt2$ is negative.

The apparent boundary mismatch cancels quadratically:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:cancel}
 -\frac{T}{a_0L}+2u\sqrt{K_\sigma}-LK_\sigma
 =-L\left(\sqrt{K_\sigma}-u/L\right)^2=O(1).
\end{equation}
Indeed~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:K} gives $K_\sigma-u^2/L^2=O(1)$, and hence the squared difference is $O(T^{-1/3})$. The Euler-sum error is $O(u/\sqrt{K_\sigma})=O(T^{1/3})$, as are all other errors in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:beforeeuler}. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:onesign}
 Q_\sigma(T)=ST+
 \frac{\zeta(1/2)}{2M\sqrt\lambda}\sqrt T+O(T^{1/3}).
\end{equation}
The signs are disjoint, and disjoint from the exceptional baseline. Adding both copies of~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:onesign} to~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:baseline} proves~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:asymptotic}, since $2S$ is the series term in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:kappa}.

This proof is unsmoothed and pointwise. The fixed-slice staircases remain present, but only $O(T^{1/3})$ short rows and columns contribute independent bounded discrepancies. No generic-position assumption, equidistribution claim, or unproved cancellation of floor phases is used.

\section{Ranked heights and binary length}\label{fup:nf:sec:inverse}
Let $F(T)=N(e^T)$, and write $d=d_*^{\mathrm{count}}$. The fiber has finitely many tuples below any finite height and is infinite, so the nondecreasing ranked heights $H_v$ exist and tend to infinity. Let $T_v=\log H_v$. Irrespective of any ties,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:ties}
 F(T_v-1)<v\le F(T_v).
\end{equation}
The lower count is strictly below the height of the $v$-th tuple; no bound on jump multiplicity is needed. Applying~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:asymptotic} at both arguments gives
\[
 v=\kappa T_v+d\sqrt{T_v}+O(T_v^{1/3}).
\]
First $T_v\sim v/\kappa$, then $T_v-v/\kappa=O(\sqrt v)$, and therefore $\sqrt{T_v}=\sqrt{v/\kappa}+O(1)$. Substituting gives~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:inverse}.

Equivalently, for a suitable fixed constant $C$ and all sufficiently large $v$,
\[
 \exp\left(\frac v\kappa-\frac d{\kappa^{3/2}}\sqrt v-Cv^{1/3}\right)
 \le H_v\le
 \exp\left(\frac v\kappa-\frac d{\kappa^{3/2}}\sqrt v+Cv^{1/3}\right).
\]
The square-root correction is positive because $d<0$. The unbounded $O(v^{1/3})$ uncertainty in the exponent is why a relative $1+o(1)$ height equivalent has not been proved.

For maximum binary coordinate length at most the integer $b\ge1$, substitute $H=2^b-1$ to obtain
\[
 N_{\mathrm{bits}}(b)=\kappa(\log2)b+
 \frac{\zeta(1/2)\sqrt{\log2}}{M\sqrt\lambda}\sqrt b+O(b^{1/3}).
\]
This is maximum bit length, not the sum of coordinate lengths; those are different size conventions.

\section{The sum of all coordinate bit lengths}\label{fup:nf:sec:totalbits}
There is a different two-term law when the budget is the sum of all twenty-two coordinate bit lengths. For $x\in\Np$ let $b(x)=\floor{\log_2x}+1$, and set
\[
 S(w)=\sum_{x\text{ a supplied coordinate of }w}b(x),\qquad
 N_{\mathrm{sum}}(B)=\#\{w\in\mathcal F:S(w)\le B\}.
\]
This is a raw unsigned coordinate-bit budget: it includes all twenty-two supplied coordinates and excludes delimiters, signs, metadata and computed registers.

\begin{theorem}[Total coordinate-bit budget]\label{fup:nf:thm:sumbits}
For fixed valid native ports and real $B\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:sumbits}
 N_{\mathrm{sum}}(B)=\kappa_s(\log2)B+
 \frac{\zeta(1/2)\sqrt{\log2}}{M\sqrt{3\lambda}}\sqrt B+O(B^{1/3}),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:ks}
 \kappa_s=\frac1{M(3p-2)\lambda}
 +\sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac1{6M\ell\beta_{M\ell}}
 =\frac\kappa3-\frac1{M\lambda(3p-2)(3p-3)}.
\end{equation}
All exact integer cutoffs and tied tuples are included.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The reconstruction formulas have the exact product cancellation
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:product}
 fijoy=\frac{R_m\,y\,(U+p)(U+c)}{c^3}.
\end{equation}
Put $\beta=\beta_m$ and $q_0=M\lambda$. The hyperbolic expressions give
\[
 \log R_m=\beta-\log2+r_m,\qquad
 \log y=(n-1)\beta+\theta_{m,n},\qquad
 \log U=(n-1)\beta+u_{m,n},
\]
with, uniformly on every admissible pair,
\[
\begin{gathered}
 0\le r_m=\log(1+e^{-2\beta})\le r_*:=\log(1+e^{-2q_0}),\\
 0\le\theta_{m,n}\le d_0:=-\log(1-e^{-2q_0}),\qquad
 -r_*\le u_{m,n}=\log(1+e^{-2n\beta})-r_m\le0.
\end{gathered}
\]
Also $U\ge1$. Taking logarithms in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:product} therefore proves
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nf:eq:prodenergy}
 \log(fijoy)=(3n-2)\beta_m+O(1)
\end{equation}
uniformly over the entire pair family. For example the absolute error is bounded by
$\log2+3\log c+3r_*+d_0+\log(1+p)+\log(1+c)$.

Define the comparison energy $E_0(m,n)=(3n-2)\beta_m$, and let $G(T)$ count admissible pairs with $E_0\le T$. For $n=p$,
\[
 E_0(M\ell,p)=(3p-2)M\lambda\ell+O(1),
\]
so its count is $T/[M(3p-2)\lambda]+O(1)$. For each sign, the exact nonbaseline energy is
\[
 E_{0,\sigma}(\ell,k)=12M\ell\beta_{M\ell}k+(3\sigma p-2)\beta_{M\ell}.
\]
Use the proof of Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:second} with $g_\ell$ replaced by $12M\ell\beta_{M\ell}$ and $a_0$ by $12\lambda M^2$. The row estimate is still $T/g_\ell+O(1)$ because the offset divided by $g_\ell$ is $(3\sigma p-2)/(12M\ell)$. The reciprocal tail is still $1/(a_0L)+O(L^{-2})$, and
$|E_{0,\sigma}-a_0k\ell^2|\le Ck\ell$ follows from~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:betabounds}. The energy is strictly increasing in both variables, so the exact sign-specific rectangle~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:hyperbola} applies unchanged. The same Euler sum and quadratic cancellation yield
\[
 G(T)=\kappa_sT+\frac{\zeta(1/2)}{M\sqrt{3\lambda}}\sqrt T+O(T^{1/3}).
\]
In particular, the exceptional baseline has coefficient $3p-2$, not $3(p-1)$, which proves the correction in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:ks}.

Let $C_{\mathrm{bits}}$ be the fixed sum of the seventeen forced bit lengths. For the five varying coordinates,
\[
 \log_2(fijoy)<b(f)+b(i)+b(j)+b(o)+b(y)
                    \le\log_2(fijoy)+5.
\]
Together with~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:prodenergy}, this gives a fixed constant $C$ for which
\[
 |(\log2)S(w)-E_0(m,n)|\le C.
\]
Hence, for every sufficiently large real $B$,
\[
 G((\log2)B-C)\le N_{\mathrm{sum}}(B)
                 \le G((\log2)B+C).
\]
Apply the asymptotic for $G$ to both sides. A fixed shift changes its linear term by $O(1)$ and its square-root term by $O(B^{-1/2})$, proving~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:sumbits}. This global sandwich includes powers of two and every rounding boundary; individual floors need not be stable.
\end{proof}

If $B_v$ is the $v$-th total bit length, in its own nondecreasing order with multiplicity, put
\[
 a_s=\kappa_s\log2,\qquad e_s=\frac{\zeta(1/2)\sqrt{\log2}}{M\sqrt{3\lambda}}.
\]
The inequalities $N_{\mathrm{sum}}(B_v-1)<v\le N_{\mathrm{sum}}(B_v)$ and the same inversion as in Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:inverse} give
\[
 B_v=\frac v{a_s}-\frac{e_s}{a_s^{3/2}}\sqrt v+O(v^{1/3}).
\]
The ordering by total bits need not agree with the ordering by maximum height. Both formulas are for their stated encoding convention.

\section{Transport to the canonical outer history}\label{fup:nf:sec:transport}
This section is a consequence of the original Report 22's entire-fiber projection theorem~\cite{fup:nf:report22}, rather than a new audit of its compiler or physical-clock construction.
\begin{writenote}
Report~22's entire-fiber projection theorem is printed with Report~22 (source~23, Part~VIII of \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}). The corollary below is conditional on it and was not re-audited in this write.
\end{writenote}

Fix an accepted nonempty input and one of its retained clock models. Determinism and first-halt semantics fix the whole trace, final payload and physical clock. The selected outer height is the unique power of two in $(S,2S]$, where $S$ is the sum of the two encoded endpoint values and the physical clock. The physical duration bounds already dominate every true quotient, so the packed trace fits at this exact height. The retained no-wrap argument recovers the exact clock from its congruence. All outer supplied coordinates and all native boundary ports are thereby fixed.

Write $w_{\mathrm{out}}$ for this unique outer tuple. Report 22 checked that the five nonnative comparisons have no native witness dependency and that the full native block is retained literally. Projection to the twenty-two native coordinates is therefore a bijection: two complete witnesses with the same native projection have the same fixed outer tuple, and adjoining $w_{\mathrm{out}}$ to any native witness makes all native and outer comparisons vanish.

\begin{corollary}[Conditional transport through the established outer bijection]
For each such accepted nonempty input, all complete witnesses are
\[
 (w_{\mathrm{out}},w_{m,n}),\qquad (m,n)\text{ as in~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:indices}}.
\]
If $C_{\mathrm{out}}$ is the maximum supplied outer coordinate, their complete height is
\[
 \max(C_{\mathrm{out}},\psi_{R_m}(n)).
\]
Their count is zero below $C_{\mathrm{out}}$ and equals $N(H)$ for $H\ge C_{\mathrm{out}}$. Thus the two-term asymptotic and logarithmic ranked-height law have the same coefficients. At finite ranks the complete height is $\max(C_{\mathrm{out}},H_v)$.
\end{corollary}
This is the only plateau possibility discussed here: it comes from additional fixed outer coordinates. It is distinct from the native height law~\eqref{fup:nf:eq:height}. The predecessor's separate no-witness initial-halt circuits are outside this nonempty-fiber corollary.

\section{Reproduction and scope}\label{fup:nf:sec:reproduction}
The packet distinguishes a mathematical proof, finite auxiliary evidence, and a sealed reproducibility artifact. Its README gives the precise commands and inventory. The core checker independently audits every literal native comparison with sparse integer-polynomial arithmetic, verifies the required pinned inputs, and uses explicit failures that remain active under optimized Python. It imports no upstream author program. Exact integer tests check the main-index divisibility equivalence, positive reconstruction, congruence residues, height domination and count values immediately below, at and above Pell-height jumps.

The second-term verification supplements the analytic proof with exact boundary tests and numerical auxiliary models. Small test values are not examples of a complete padded native tuple unless the native hypotheses have separately been checked; the astronomical native tuple has not been materialized. Floating-point agreement is never used to prove the classification, floors at a boundary, or the asymptotic theorem.

The release includes source provenance, independently reviewed mathematical statements, portable checker adaptations, deterministic receipts and a strict manifest. Replays run the substantive checks in both normal and optimized Python, test rejection of altered or missing required material, and compare the entire release inventory and bytes before and after. A fresh extracted ZIP is the final replay target. The PDF build requires a local LaTeX installation; the verification code itself uses only the Python standard library. Source hashes and any portable text/code adaptation hashes are kept distinct in the provenance record.

\subsection{Attribution and limits}
Pell generation, strong divisibility, step-down congruences, fixed-base logarithmic counting and Euler--Maclaurin summation are classical. The native repository already contains the crucial relaxed-norm integrality recovery, fixed-minus necessity and parity analysis, and a positive converse. The present compiler-specific deduction is the exact seventeen/five split, the full two-index parameter set with its converse, native maximum-coordinate domination, the baseline-corrected whole-fiber coefficient, and the pointwise two-term count for that exact fiber.

These statements do not change the native circuit, add an uncharged operation, prove circuit minimality, establish a general finite-fold MRDP result, or produce a full numerical Pell witness. Nor is a literature-wide open-problem or novelty claim made. The asymptotic parameters are fixed: neither the error constants nor its onset are asserted uniform across different external inputs.

Binary powering gives effective evaluation and exact comparison in the two quadratic rings. One can locate integer cutoffs by monotone searches rather than floating-point inversion. This is computability, not a small-witness claim: a coordinate at normalized index $n$ requires $\Theta(n\log\varepsilon_m)$ output bits, and the fixed native coordinates can already be enormous. The least-height pair and exact counts are mathematical descriptions; they do not implement a canonical choice by the retained polynomial.

\subsection{Further mathematical questions}
Several bounded questions remain. Can the $O((\log H)^{1/3})$ error be improved, or can further oscillatory terms be identified without smoothing? Can the onset and constants be controlled uniformly while the external ports vary? What is the cost of enforcing a preferred auxiliary-index choice in the retained arithmetic model, with every operation charged? How do other explicit encodings change the coefficient and secondary term? None of these improvements is claimed here.
