\fupstart{34}{Part IV: Report 34}
\fupsourcehead{Report 34. Height and Counting of a Signed19 Family: canonical witnesses with negative restored index}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~05 of batch~82: \emph{Research Report 34}, author line ``Mathematical research report'', dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Counterexample_Height_Expansions_and_Rotation_Discrepancy_Package.zip}; pins \texttt{2dde7850f} (construction) and \texttt{9fa99be93} (transfer appendix). Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 34.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
We fix the input, compiler export, repunit and one prime-scale choice in the signed19 construction of Report 33, and count only its prescribed canonical auxiliary witnesses. Each main index in one Chinese-remainder progression admits at most one first-Pell index in the required strict ratio window. The maximum of the nineteen supplied coordinates is eventually the last auxiliary Pell coordinate, whose double logarithm has an explicit exponentially corrected expansion and a Lambert $W$ inverse. The height count satisfies $N(B)=\kappa\log\log B+O((\log\log B)^{1-\varepsilon})$ for some effective fixed-parameter $\varepsilon>0$. A logarithmic-form bound makes the exact selection eventually identical to a fixed irrational-rotation test. The resulting exact discrepancy term is unbounded by Kesten's theorem, ruling out a smooth two-term count with bounded remainder. The finer two-term law with $o(\log\log\log B)$ remainder is equivalent to a specific unresolved $o(\log R)$ discrepancy estimate. This is a theorem about one explicit subfamily, not the whole signed19 fiber.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
Report~34 has no executable mathematical checker by design and has not been reviewed by the programme. Its letters differ from Part~III's: here $\lambda=A+\sqrt\Delta$ and $\alpha=\log\lambda$; $\kappa$ is a counting coefficient and $\kin$ is Report~33's $\kappa$; $\asrc,\dsrc$ are Report~33's coordinates $\alpha,\delta$; and its paid mask is written $\MFsrc$ as in Report~33. The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{05-signed19-heights-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{The result and the object being counted}\label{fup:hc:sec:result}
Report 33 proves that, for every positive ordinary input and every actual fixed compiler export in its pinned source, the complete74 signed19 index-eliminated child has infinitely many positive supplied-witness zeros with negative restored index~\cite{fup:hc:r33}. We study the size and number of one canonical family within that construction. Report 33 and its source are unchanged.

The term \emph{negative restoration} means that the omitted, algebraically computed index is $R=-p<0$. It does not mean that any of the nineteen supplied coordinates is negative. A negative computed intermediate is allowed by the signed presentation. Nothing here identifies a machine input's acceptance status or changes a positive-parent theorem.

Fix all construction data in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:family}. In particular the prime used to choose the scale is fixed once and for all. Write
\begin{gather}\label{fup:hc:eq:constants}
 \Delta=A^2-1,\qquad D_P=P^2-1,\qquad
 \lambda=A+\sqrt\Delta,\qquad \nu=P+\sqrt{D_P},\\
 \alpha=\log\lambda,\qquad \beta=\log\nu,\qquad
 L=E/2,\qquad V=TQH_0,\notag\\
 \delta=\log(1+1/Y),\qquad
 \drot=\frac{\delta}{L\beta},\qquad
 \kappa=\frac{\delta}{2\alpha V L\beta},\qquad
 C_0=\frac{\alpha}{2\Delta}.\notag
\end{gather}
All logarithms are natural. The growth logarithm $\alpha$ is distinct from the source coordinate $\asrc$; $\delta$ and $\kappa$ likewise differ from $\dsrc$ and $\kin$. The letter $B$ below is a height bound, whereas $B_{\mathrm{comp}}$ denotes the fixed compiler radix.

For each eligible pair $(p,n)$, let $\mathbf w(p,n)$ be the full tuple in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:family}, and put
\[
 \htop(\mathbf w)=\max\{\text{its nineteen supplied coordinates}\}.
\]
We count a sufficiently late tail $\mathcal C$ of these tuples, retaining all pairs in that tail which satisfy the exact congruence and strict ratio test. Define
\[
 N(B)=\#\{\mathbf w\in\mathcal C:\htop(\mathbf w)\le B\}.
\]
The tail begins at one fixed main-index progression representative $p_b$. Its onset may be enlarged to ensure positivity, height dominance and the exact rotation reduction. Finite changes to this convention affect $N$ by $O(1)$ only.

\begin{theorem}[Canonical family height and count]\label{fup:hc:thm:main}
For the fixed data just specified, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Each $p=p_b+Vr$, $r\in\Z_{\ge0}$, has at most one positive $n\equiv n_0\pmod L$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:ratio}
 Y<\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}<Y+1.
\end{equation}
The proportion of progression terms admitting such an $n$ is $\drot>0$.
\item On $\mathcal C$, the height is exactly $y=\psi_{\qaux}(S)$ and strictly increases with $p$. With $b_0=(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1$,
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:heightmain}
 \log y&=C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}\bigl(1+O(e^{-\alpha p})\bigr),\\
 \log\log y&=2\alpha p+2\log p+\log C_0
   +\sqrt\Delta\left(1+\frac{b_0}{p}\right)e^{-\alpha p}
   +O(e^{-2\alpha p}).\label{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}
\end{align}
\item If $B_j$ is the $j$th height in increasing order, then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:leading}
 N(B)\sim\kappa\log\log B,\qquad
 \log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa.
\end{equation}
For some effectively computable $\varepsilon>0$ depending on the fixed data,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:effective}
 N(B)=\kappa\log\log B+O\bigl((\log\log B)^{1-\varepsilon}\bigr),\quad
 \log\log B_j=\frac j\kappa+O(j^{1-\varepsilon}).
\end{equation}
\item There is an exact fixed-rotation discrepancy $D(R)$, defined in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:exact}, such that, for $t=\log\log B$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:decomposition}
 N(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+D(R(B))+O(1),\qquad
 R(B)\sim\frac{t}{2\alpha V}.
\end{equation}
The smooth all-height formula $N(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+O(1)$ is false. The weaker formula with $o(\log t)$ in place of $O(1)$ holds if and only if $D(R)=o(\log R)$; that condition is not established here.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
The height inverse is given more precisely in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:inverse}. All constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed input, compiler, repunit and prime-scale choice. No parameter-uniform exponent or useful numerical onset is asserted. Equations~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:leading} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective} are statements on the double-logarithmic scale, not multiplicative equivalents for the heights.

\section{The canonical family and its source interface}\label{fup:hc:sec:family}
This section defines every tuple being counted. We inherit from the audited construction in Report 33 its integrality, positivity on a sufficiently late tail, and satisfaction of the full child equations. We recall the exact formulas and the relevant hypotheses rather than reproducing its whole existence proof.

\subsection{Fixed compiler and scale choices}
For an integer $D\ge2$ define the Pell sequences by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:pell}
 \chi_D(v)+\psi_D(v)\sqrt{D^2-1}
   =\bigl(D+\sqrt{D^2-1}\bigr)^v\qquad(v\in\Z_{\ge0}).
\end{equation}
Their norm is $\chi_D(v)^2-(D^2-1)\psi_D(v)^2=1$.

Fix $x\in\pos$ and an actual compiler export from Report 33: $B_{\mathrm{comp}}=2^d\ge16$, with $d$ a power of five, $b$ positive and odd, $K_0\ge0$, and the fixed masks $M_C,M_F^{\mathrm{native}}$. Put $\MFsrc=M_F^{\mathrm{native}}+B_{\mathrm{comp}}-1$. All further compiler requirements remain those of the inherited source; no toy mask replaces these numerals.

Choose and fix a power of five $N_0$ such that $q=B_{\mathrm{comp}}^{N_0}>2dx+1$. Define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:scale1}
 t_0&=dN_0,&p_0&=12t_0+7,& X&=2^{p_0},&Q&=q^2-1,\notag\\
 J&=\frac{q-1}{B_{\mathrm{comp}}-1},&w&=2^{p_0-3t_0},&
 \asrc&=q-1-2dx,&z&=1,\\
 F&=K_0+X-q+1,&M&=(M_C+q\MFsrc)J,& C&=1.&\notag
\end{align}
Thus $q=2^{t_0}$ and $X=wq^3$. Set $D_0=4q^3(X+1)/3$. The construction proves $D_0\in\pos$ and $\gcd(D_0,Q)=1$. Choose $s_0$ with
\[
 \gcd(s_0,Q)=\gcd(1+D_0s_0,Q)=1,
\]
and fix one prime $\ell>3$ in the primitive progression $1+D_0s_0\pmod{D_0Q}$, large enough that $s=(\ell-1)/D_0>0$. Dirichlet's theorem supplies such primes; this report neither varies nor counts them. Define
\begin{gather}\label{fup:hc:eq:scale2}
 Y=sq^3,\quad E=XY,\quad a=Y(X+1),\quad A=a+2,\quad
 H_0=4a+3=3\ell,\\
 \Delta=A^2-1=a^2+H_0,\qquad P=2XY^2+1,\notag\\
 O=\ord_{H_0}(2),\qquad T=\lcm(4,2E,O),\qquad L=E/2.\notag
\end{gather}
The inherited scale argument gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:crtcompat}
 \gcd(QH_0,T)=1.
\end{equation}
This fact is what allows a free main-index progression after imposing all outer constraints.

\subsection{Indices and all nineteen coordinates}
The positive odd input index $u=2dx+b\ge3$ is fixed. Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:inputfixed}
 \kin=\psi_A(u),\qquad \mu_0=\chi_A(u),\qquad
 e_u=\mu_0-a\kin,\qquad \dsrc=\frac{\kin-u}{\Delta}.
\end{equation}
Choose $p$ in the single simultaneous progression
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:crt}
 p\equiv p_0\pmod T,\qquad
 p\equiv-M-Q(q^2-qF+e_u-1)\pmod{QH_0}.
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:crtcompat}, its step is $V=TQH_0$. In particular $p\equiv3\pmod4$. Fix $n_0\equiv(1-p_0)/2\pmod L$. Retain the pairs $(p,n)$ with $n\in\pos$, $n\equiv n_0\pmod L$, and the exact test~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:ratio}.

For each retained pair define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:maincoords}
 c&=\psi_A(p),&D&=\chi_A(p),&k&=2\psi_P(n),&\tau&=\chi_P(n),\notag\\
 \eta&=c-kY,&\zeta&=k-\eta,&h&=\frac{k+p-1}{E},&R&=-p,\\
 Z&=q^2-qF+\frac{p+M}{Q},&\rho&=\frac{Z+e_u-1}{H_0},&&&\notag\\
 \gamma&=\frac{D-ac-X}{H_0},&\sigma&=\gamma-\rho,&W&=1-Z.&&\notag
\end{align}
The \emph{canonical auxiliary choice} in this report is exactly
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:auxcoords}
 m&=pc,&f&=\chi_A(m),&\qaux&=\Delta\psi_A(m),&i&=\frac{\qaux}{c^2},\notag\\
 S&=p+2m,&U&=\frac{\chi_{\qaux}(S)}{\qaux},&y&=\psi_{\qaux}(S),&&\\
 j&=\frac{U-p}{c},&o&=\frac{U+c}{f}.&&&&\notag
\end{align}
The supplied tuple, in source order, is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:tuple}
 \mathbf w(p,n)=(J,F,\asrc,z,f,h,i,j,o,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,y,Z,\dsrc,\rho,\sigma).
\end{equation}
All its coordinates are positive integers for sufficiently large eligible $p$. This is the construction theorem inherited from Report 33, Sections 4--9, and its independent audit~\cite{fup:hc:construction,fup:hc:audit33}. In particular, $\qaux/c^2$, $U$, $j$, and $o$ are integers by the divisibility and odd-quotient Pell identities proved there. Their integrality is not inferred from the asymptotic analysis below.
\begin{writenote}
Report~33's Sections~4--9 are Sections~\ref{fup:ir:sec:scale}--\ref{fup:ir:sec:conclusion} of this Part. The construction packet and audit cited here are shipped once, as source~10's \rw{10-index-restore-repro-evidence-FULL-SIGNED-COUNTEREXAMPLE.md} and \rw{10-index-restore-repro-evidence-INDEPENDENT-AUDIT.md}; source~05's copies, \rw{evidence/FULL-SIGNED-COUNTEREXAMPLE.md} and \rw{evidence/CONSTRUCTION-INDEPENDENT-AUDIT.md}, are byte-identical and not shipped.
\end{writenote}

There is no choice of a second auxiliary index, a different multiple of $pc$, another prime, or a new scale hidden in $N(B)$. Once $(p,n)$ is selected, every entry of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple} is prescribed. Distinct $p$ already yield distinct tuples because $Z$ is affine in $p$ with positive slope.

\subsection{Link to the complete child equations}
For clarity, the child consists of the sum of squares of the following eight residuals, with the computed definitions in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:scale1}--\eqref{fup:hc:eq:auxcoords} and $\mu=W+a\kin+\rho H_0$:
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:child}
 (K_0+X)C&=F+z(q-1),\notag\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)(q^2-1)+(M_C+q\MFsrc)J,\notag\\
 \tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2&=1,\qquad D^2-\Delta c^2=1,\notag\\
 (ic^2)^2&=\Delta(f^2-1),\\
 (ic^2)^2\bigl((jc-R)^2-y^2\bigr)&=1-y^2,\notag\\
 jc-R&=of-c,\qquad \mu^2-\Delta\kin^2=1.\notag
\end{align}
The restored expression is $R=k-hE-1=-p$. Also $c=kY+\eta$, $\gamma=\rho+\sigma$, $D=X+ac+\gamma H_0$, $\kin=u+\dsrc\Delta$, and $\mu=\mu_0>0$. Thus the family satisfies the whole signed19 interface, rather than just an auxiliary subsystem. The validity and positivity proof of these statements is the inherited theorem. Only the size and count of this prescribed family are newly analyzed here.

\section{The exact first index window}\label{fup:hc:sec:window}
The two quadratic fields are distinct. Indeed $A$ is even, so $\Delta$ is odd, while
\[
 D_P=4XY^2(XY^2+1)
\]
has odd $2$-adic valuation $2+p_0+2v_2(Y)$. The squarefree part of $D_P$ is therefore even and that of $\Delta$ is odd. Both define real quadratic fields, and their intersection is $\Q$. Consequently $\alpha/\beta$ is irrational: a rational relation would put equal positive powers of $\lambda$ and $\nu$ in that intersection, and the norm-one property would force such a positive rational power to be $1$, which is impossible.

Put $c_p=\psi_A(p)$ and
\[
 g=\log\frac{\sqrt{D_P}}{2\sqrt\Delta}.
\]
The hyperbolic Pell formulas make~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:ratio} exactly equivalent to
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:window}
 a_p<n\beta<b_p,\qquad
 a_p=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2(Y+1)},\qquad
 b_p=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2Y}.
\end{equation}
The derivative of $t\mapsto\arsinh(e^t)$ lies strictly between $0$ and $1$. Integrating across a logarithmic interval of width $\delta$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:width}
 0<b_p-a_p<\delta<L\beta.
\end{equation}
Here $\delta\le\log2$, $L\ge1$, and $\beta=\arcosh P>\log2$. Distinct allowed $n$ have spacing $L$, so at most one can satisfy~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:window}. This is an exact uniqueness result, before any limiting approximation. Both endpoints tend to infinity, and any large-index solution has
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:nsize}
 n=(\alpha/\beta)p+O(1)>0.
\end{equation}

For $v\in\{Y,Y+1\}$ define
\[
 \epsilon_v(p)=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2v}
       -(\alpha p+g-\log v).
\]
Binet's formula and $\arsinh z=\log(2z)+(4z^2)^{-1}+O(z^{-4})$ give
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:endpoint}
 \epsilon_v(p)=\left(\frac{4v^2\Delta}{D_P}-1\right)e^{-2\alpha p}
                    +O(e^{-4\alpha p}).
\end{equation}
The $-1$ term is the Binet correction. It is essential to the first endpoint coefficient.

Write the main progression as $p=p_b+Vr$, $r\ge0$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:rotation}
 \theta=\frac{V\alpha}{L\beta},\qquad
 \xi=\frac{\alpha p_b+g-\log Y-n_0\beta}{L\beta},\qquad
 I=(0,\drot)\pmod1.
\end{equation}
The limiting interval contains a point of $n_0\beta+L\beta\Z$ precisely when
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:limtest}
 \{\xi+r\theta\}\in I.
\end{equation}
To check the phase convention, put $z=(\alpha p+g-\log Y-n_0\beta)/(L\beta)$. The condition is that some integer lies strictly between $z-\drot$ and $z$, equivalent to $0<\{z\}<\drot$.

Because $\theta$ is irrational, the proportion of fixed-interval hits is $\drot$. This transfers to the exact moving window without a logarithmic-form theorem: for any $\epsilon>0$, after finitely many indices the exact and limiting indicators can differ only within $\epsilon$-neighborhoods of the two endpoints of $I$. Equidistribution bounds the upper density of these discrepancies by $4\epsilon$. Letting $\epsilon$ tend to zero proves assertion (i) and positive density. This argument alone does not imply finitely many discrepancies; Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:exact} supplies that stronger fact separately.

\section{The maximum supplied coordinate and its growth}\label{fup:hc:sec:height}
\subsection{Eventual exact height and no ties}
The strict ratio gives $k<c/Y$, hence $\eta,\zeta=O(c)$ and $\tau=O(c)$ by the first norm. Also $h=O(c+p)$; $Z$ and $\rho$ are affine in $p$; and $\sigma=O(c+p)$ by the formula for $\gamma$. The remaining nonauxiliary coordinates in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple} are fixed. All these estimates have fixed-data implied constants.

For the auxiliary coordinates, $\qaux=ic^2$ with $i\ge1$, and
\[
 \qaux^2=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 y\ge\psi_{\qaux}(3)=4\qaux^2-1>\qaux\ge c^2.
\]
Since $\Delta\ge3$ and $f\ge2$, we have $f<\qaux$ and $i\le\qaux$. The auxiliary norm is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:unorm}
 U^2=(1-\qaux^{-2})y^2+\qaux^{-2}<y^2.
\end{equation}
Therefore $j=(U-p)/c<y$ and
\[
 o=\frac{U+c}{f}<\frac{y+c}{2}<y.
\]
Finally $y\ge4c^4-1$ dominates every $O(c+p+1)$ coordinate because $c$ grows exponentially and $p=o(c)$. This checks all nineteen entries of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple}. Thus $\htop(\mathbf w)=y$ with strict dominance on a sufficiently late tail.

Extend the formulas to real $p\ge1$ by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:realextension}
 \begin{gathered}
 c=\frac{\sinh(\alpha p)}{\sqrt\Delta},\quad m=pc,\quad
 \qaux=\sqrt\Delta\sinh(\alpha m),\quad S=p+2m,\\
 b_{\mathrm{aux}}=\arcosh\qaux,\quad
 y(p)=\frac{\sinh(Sb_{\mathrm{aux}})}{\sinh b_{\mathrm{aux}}}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Here $c(1)=m(1)=1$, $\qaux(1)=\Delta\ge3$, and $S(1)=3$. The functions $m$, $\qaux$, and $S$ increase strictly. For $S>1$ and $b>0$, $\sinh(Sb)/\sinh b$ increases in both arguments: the derivative of its logarithm in $b$ is $S\coth(Sb)-\coth b>0$, because $z\coth z$ increases on $(0,\infty)$. Thus $y(p)$ is strictly increasing, and the canonical family has no height ties after the fixed tail onset.

\subsection{An exponentially corrected expansion}
Put $d_0=\sqrt\Delta$ and $\ell_0=\log d_0$. From~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:realextension},
\[
 b_{\mathrm{aux}}=\alpha m+\ell_0+O(e^{-2\alpha m}),\qquad
 \log(2\sinh b_{\mathrm{aux}})=\alpha m+\ell_0+O(e^{-2\alpha m}).
\]
The logarithm of the numerator in $y$ contributes the remaining term $\log(1-e^{-2Sb_{\mathrm{aux}}})$, which is smaller. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:logym}
 \log y=(2m+p-1)(\alpha m+\ell_0)
                 +O\bigl((m+p)e^{-2\alpha m}\bigr).
\end{equation}
The error is superexponentially small in $p$. Substituting
\[
 m=\frac{p}{2d_0}e^{\alpha p}(1-e^{-2\alpha p})
\]
gives the first two scales explicitly:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:twoscales}
 \log y=C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}
   +\frac{p}{2d_0}\bigl[\alpha(p-1)+\log\Delta\bigr]e^{\alpha p}
   +O(p^2).
\end{equation}
Divide by $C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}$ and take the logarithm. The resulting first correction is
\[
 d_0\left(1+\frac{(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1}{p}\right)e^{-\alpha p},
\]
and the rest is $O(e^{-2\alpha p})$. This proves~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightmain}--\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}, including the coefficient $C_0=\alpha/(2\Delta)$.

\section{The real height cutoff and Lambert inversion}\label{fup:hc:sec:inverse}
Strict monotonicity gives, for every sufficiently large $B$, a unique real $p_B$ with $y(p_B)=B$. The exact height condition on the tail is $p\le p_B$.

Let $W$ be the positive real branch of the Lambert function, satisfying $W(z)e^{W(z)}=z$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:lambert}
 t_B=\frac1\alpha W\bigl(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B}\bigr).
\end{equation}
It solves the leading model exactly:
\[
 C_0t_B^2e^{2\alpha t_B}=\log B,\qquad
 2\alpha t_B+2\log t_B+\log C_0=\log\log B.
\]
Write $b_0=(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1$. Equation~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}, with the positive derivative $2\alpha+2/p$ of the leading logarithmic model, first brackets $p_B-t_B$ by $O(e^{-\alpha t_B})$. Taylor expansion of the displayed smooth correction then gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:fineinverse}
 p_B=t_B-
 \frac{\sqrt\Delta(1+b_0/t_B)e^{-\alpha t_B}}{2\alpha+2/t_B}
       +O(e^{-2\alpha t_B}).
\end{equation}
The derivative of the first correction is $O(e^{-\alpha t_B})$ in this neighborhood; multiplying by the first bracket error produces the stated $O(e^{-2\alpha t_B})$ residual. In particular $p_B<t_B$ eventually.

A coarser expression is
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:inverseexp}
 p_B&=\frac1\alpha W\bigl(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B}\bigr)
   +O\left(e^{-W(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B})}\right)\notag\\
 &=\frac{\log\log B-2\log\log\log B+\log(8\alpha\Delta)}{2\alpha}
     +O\left(\frac{\log\log\log B}{\log\log B}\right).
\end{align}
The latter follows either by expanding $W$ or by substitution into the strictly increasing leading model. It is an inverse-height expansion, not a counting second term.

The difference $|p_B-t_B|$ is eventually smaller than $V$, so using $t_B$ instead of $p_B$ in a progression cutoff changes the count by at most one. It need not give equality at every jump. We keep $p_B$ in the exact floor formula below.

The density in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:window} already proves
\[
 N(B)\sim\frac{\drot}{V}p_B\sim\kappa\log\log B.
\]
If $p_j$ is the $j$th selected main index, then $p_j\sim Vj/\drot$. Inserting this in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine} proves $\log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa$. Only the double logarithm is substituted; an $o(p_j)$ index error must not be silently exponentiated into a multiplicative height equivalent.

% All-orders height and inverse refinement
\section{Height and cutoff expansions to every order}\label{fup:hc:sec:allorders}
The first correction above admits an all-orders refinement. It affects the continuous height and its inverse, without improving the rotation discrepancy. It is useful to separate a convergent analytic \emph{normal form} from the smaller remainder of the exact quantity.

Put $d_0=\sqrt\Delta$, $\epsilon=e^{-\alpha p}$, and define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:AB}
 A_p=d_0(1-1/p),\qquad B_p=\frac{d_0\log\Delta}{\alpha p},\qquad
 G_0(p)=2\alpha p+2\log p+\log C_0.
\end{equation}
The symbols $A_p,B_p$ here are coefficient functions, distinct from the fixed Pell base $A$ and the height bound $B$.

\begin{proposition}[All orders at fixed parameters]\label{fup:hc:prop:allorders}
For each fixed integer $N\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:allheight}
 \log\log y(p)=G_0(p)+\sum_{n=1}^N a_n(p)e^{-n\alpha p}
                         +O(e^{-(N+1)\alpha p}),
\end{equation}
where $a_n(p)$ is a polynomial in $1/p$ of degree at most $n$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:coefficientrec}
 S_0(C)=2,\quad S_1(C)=-C,\quad
 S_n(C)=-CS_{n-1}(C)+S_{n-2}(C),\quad
 a_n(p)=-\frac{S_n(A_p)+S_n(B_p)}n.
\end{equation}
For $t=t_B$ from~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:lambert}, the exact cutoff has, for every fixed $N$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:allinverse}
 p_B=t+\sum_{n=1}^N c_n(t)e^{-n\alpha t}
                  +O(e^{-(N+1)\alpha t}).
\end{equation}
Each $c_n(t)$ is rational in $t$ and the fixed constants, bounded as $t\to\infty$, and analytic as a function of $1/t$ near zero. A triangular recurrence is given in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:inverserec}. Constants may depend on $N$ and all the fixed data.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The leading expression $F(p)=(2m+p-1)(\alpha m+\tfrac12\log\Delta)$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:logym} has the exact factorization
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:factorization}
 F(p)=C_0p^2\epsilon^{-2}
       (1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2).
\end{equation}
Indeed, $m=p(\epsilon^{-1}-\epsilon)/(2d_0)$, so the two original factors respectively equal $(p/d_0)\epsilon^{-1}(1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)$ and $(\alpha p/(2d_0))\epsilon^{-1}(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)$. Since $F(p)\sim2\alpha m^2$ and $m\ge p$, dividing the error in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:logym} by $F(p)$ and taking a logarithm gives
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:normalform}
 \log\log y(p)&=G_0(p)+\log(1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)
                  +\log(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)+\mathcal R(p),\notag\\
 \mathcal R(p)&=O(e^{-2\alpha m}/m)=o(e^{-K\alpha p})
                                  \quad\text{for every fixed }K>0.
\end{align}

The roots $r_\pm(C)=(-C\pm\sqrt{C^2+4})/2$ satisfy
\[
 1+C\epsilon-\epsilon^2=(1-r_+(C)\epsilon)(1-r_-(C)\epsilon).
\]
Their power sums are $S_n(C)$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:coefficientrec}, and hence
\[
 \log(1+C\epsilon-\epsilon^2)
       =-\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{S_n(C)}n\epsilon^n
\]
whenever $|\epsilon|\max(|r_+|,|r_-|)<1$, taking branches that vanish at zero. For $p\ge1$, both $A_p$ and $B_p$ lie in a fixed bounded nonnegative interval. A constant $K_0'\ge1$ therefore bounds all four root moduli uniformly. For $|\epsilon|\le1/(2K_0')$, the sum of the two tails after order $N$ has absolute value at most
\[
 4\sum_{n>N}\frac{(K_0'|\epsilon|)^n}{n}
 \le\frac{8(K_0')^{N+1}}{N+1}|\epsilon|^{N+1}.
\]
This proves uniform convergence of the two-logarithm normal form. The recurrence shows that $S_n$ is a polynomial of degree at most $n$, proving the coefficient assertion and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:allheight}.

For inversion put $p=t+u$ and $w_0=e^{-\alpha t}$. Define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:Hnormal}
 H_t(u,w_0)={}&2\alpha u+2\log(1+u/t)\notag\\
 &+\log\bigl(1+A_{t+u}w_0e^{-\alpha u}-w_0^2e^{-2\alpha u}\bigr)\notag\\
 &+\log\bigl(1+B_{t+u}w_0e^{-\alpha u}-w_0^2e^{-2\alpha u}\bigr).
\end{align}
Introduce $z_0=1/t$, replacing $1/(t+u)$ by $z_0/(1+z_0u)$. The resulting function $H(u,z_0,w_0)$ is jointly analytic near $(0,0,0)$, using logarithms near $1$. It satisfies
\[
 H(0,z_0,0)=0,\qquad \partial_uH(0,z_0,0)=2\alpha+2z_0,
\]
which is nonzero near zero. The analytic implicit-function theorem supplies a unique analytic small root $\mathcal U(z_0,w_0)$ on a fixed neighborhood, with $\mathcal U(z_0,0)=0$. Its series in $w_0$ converges uniformly on a smaller polydisk and has uniformly bounded fixed-order remainders.

Explicitly, write $u_{<n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}c_j(t)w_0^j$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:inverserec}
 c_n(t)=-\frac{[w_0^n]H_t(u_{<n},w_0)}{2\alpha+2/t}.
\end{equation}
Adding $c_n(t)w_0^n$ affects the coefficient of order $n$ only through the displayed linear derivative. Expansion of the rational functions, exponentials and logarithms proves inductively that these coefficients are rational in $t$ and the fixed constants; joint analyticity proves regularity and boundedness at $1/t=0$.

It remains to justify inversion for the exact height, rather than just its normal form. The already proved first-order inverse gives $u_{\mathrm{ex}}=p_B-t=O(w_0)$. Equation~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:normalform} gives
\[
 H_t(u_{\mathrm{ex}},w_0)=-\mathcal R(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}}).
\]
In this real neighborhood $\partial_uH_t\ge\alpha>0$ for large $t$. The mean value theorem bounds the distance from $u_{\mathrm{ex}}$ to $\mathcal U(1/t,w_0)$ by $\alpha^{-1}|\mathcal R(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}})|$. Since $m(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}})/m(t)\to1$, this is
\[
 O(e^{-\alpha m(t)}/m(t))=o(w_0^K)\quad\text{for every fixed }K>0.
\]
The uniformly convergent normal-form series therefore gives the actual fixed-order expansion~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:allinverse}.
\end{proof}

For reference, $a_1=A_p+B_p$ and $a_2=-2-(A_p^2+B_p^2)/2$, while
\[
 c_1(t)=-\frac{d_0[1+((\log\Delta)/\alpha-1)/t]}{2\alpha+2/t}.
\]
These reproduce the earlier first corrections. The convergent series sum to the \emph{normal forms}; the exact height and cutoff still differ by beyond-all-orders terms. Neither series is asserted to equal the exact quantity. Smooth expansions of arbitrarily high fixed order do not remove the counting discrepancy analyzed next.


\section{An eventual exact rotation count}\label{fup:hc:sec:exact}
We now strengthen the density argument using a standard effective theorem on linear forms in logarithms. For fixed positive algebraic numbers $z_1,\ldots,z_s$, there are effective constants $c,C>0$, depending on those numbers, such that every nonzero real form
\[
 \Lambda=\sum_{i=1}^s b_i\log z_i,\qquad b_i\in\Z,
\]
satisfies $|\Lambda|\ge c\mathcal B^{-C}$, where $\mathcal B=\max(3,|b_1|,\ldots,|b_s|)$. The sufficient statement is Theorem 1.1, equation (1.2), and its fixed-number consequence in Bugeaud~\cite{fup:hc:bugeaud}; Baker--W\"ustholz~\cite{fup:hc:baker} is a classical additional reference. No multiplicative-independence assumption is needed beyond nonvanishing of the particular form.

For $v=Y,Y+1$ and an integer $n$ near~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:nsize}, consider
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:boundaryform}
 \Lambda_v(p,n)=p\log\lambda-n\log\nu+
           \log\frac{\sqrt{D_P}}{2v\sqrt\Delta}.
\end{equation}
This form is nonzero. If it vanished, squaring the associated positive multiplicative equality would give
\[
 \lambda^{2p}=\frac{4v^2\Delta}{D_P}\nu^{2n}.
\]
Its left side lies in $\Q(\sqrt\Delta)$ and its right side in $\Q(\sqrt{D_P})$, hence both are rational. The norm-one property forces the positive rational $\lambda^{2p}$ to equal $1$, contrary to $p>0$.

The coefficients in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:boundaryform} are $O(p)$ by~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:nsize}. Thus, uniformly for either endpoint and all relevant large $p$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:boundarylower}
 |\Lambda_v(p,n)|\ge c_1p^{-C_1}
\end{equation}
for effective fixed-data constants. This eventually exceeds the $O(e^{-2\alpha p})$ endpoint shifts in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:endpoint}. A disagreement between an exact and limiting indicator would require an allowed lattice point within that exponentially small distance of a limiting endpoint, contradicting~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:boundarylower}. Therefore only finitely many disagreements occur.

Enlarge $p_b$ within its progression beyond these exceptions and the positivity and height thresholds. Use the phase $\xi$ corresponding to this new $p_b$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:rotation}. For every $r\ge0$, the exact selection is now precisely~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:limtest}. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:discrepancy}
 D(R)=\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\mathbf 1_I(\{\xi+r\theta\})-\drot R
       \qquad(R\in\Z_{\ge0}),
\end{equation}
with the empty sum zero. In $D(R)$ and $R(B)$, $R$ denotes a nonnegative number of progression terms, not the restored coordinate $R=-p$ used in the construction. For all sufficiently large $B$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:Rcut}
 R(B)=\max\left(0,1+\left\lfloor\frac{p_B-p_b}{V}\right\rfloor\right).
\end{equation}
The exact identity for this tail is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:exactcount}
 N(B)=\drot R(B)+D(R(B)).
\end{equation}
This retains the fixed phase, the open strict-ratio interval and the true real cutoff. No asymptotic expression has been passed through a floor as if exact.

Combining~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:inverseexp} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:Rcut} gives, for $t=\log\log B$,
\[
 \drot R(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+O(1),\qquad
 R(B)\sim t/(2\alpha V),
\]
which proves~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition}. Changing to another finite initial convention merely adds a fixed count offset and does not remove the discrepancy.

\section{Effective errors and the second term obstruction}\label{fup:hc:sec:errors}
\subsection{A fixed parameter power saving}
For $h\ge1$, choose an integer $k_h$ nearest to $h\theta$. The nonzero form
\[
 hV\alpha-k_hL\beta
\]
has integer coefficients $O(h)$, and its nonvanishing follows from irrationality of $\theta$. The same logarithmic-form bound therefore supplies effective $c_2>0$ and $\mu\ge1$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:diophantine}
 \|h\theta\|\ge c_2h^{-\mu}.
\end{equation}
The exponent can be weakened to ensure $\mu\ge1$. The finite geometric sum gives
\[
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}e^{2\pi ih(\xi+r\theta)}\right|
 \le\min\left(R,\frac1{2\|h\theta\|}\right)\ll h^\mu.
\]
The unnormalized Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an inequality~\cite{fup:hc:et1,fup:hc:et2}, with an absolute implied constant, yields for each integer $K\ge1$
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:ET}
 |D(R)|&\ll\frac{R}{K+1}+
 \sum_{h=1}^K\frac1h
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}e^{2\pi ih(\xi+r\theta)}\right|\notag\\
 &\ll\frac RK+K^\mu.
\end{align}
Taking $K$ of order $R^{1/(\mu+1)}$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:powersaving}
 D(R)=O(R^{1-\varepsilon}),\qquad \varepsilon=\frac1{\mu+1}>0.
\end{equation}
Since $R(B)$ is of order $t$ and $\varepsilon\le1/2$, the analytic $\log t$ correction is absorbed by this error. Equations~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:exactcount} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} prove the counting part of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective}.

There are no height ties, so $N(B_j)=j$. The leading law gives $t_j=\log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa$; substituting this into $j=\kappa t_j+O(t_j^{1-\varepsilon})$ proves the ranked error in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective}. This effective error may be very weak, and remains much larger than the scale $\log\log\log B$ of the putative second term.

\subsection{Why a bounded smooth remainder is impossible}
Kesten's interval bounded-remainder theorem states that an interval for an irrational rotation has bounded discrepancy if and only if its length belongs to $\Z+\theta\Z$~\cite{fup:hc:kesten,fup:hc:kelly}. It applies to a fixed phase: translating the interval absorbs that phase. Open versus half-open endpoint conventions change at most two orbit hits.

In our case $\drot\notin\Z+\theta\Z$. Otherwise integers $a_1,b_1$ would give
\[
 \delta=a_1L\beta+b_1V\alpha,\qquad
 \frac{Y+1}{Y}=\nu^{a_1L}\lambda^{b_1V}.
\]
The numbers $\lambda$ and $\nu$ and their inverses are algebraic integers, since their monic integer polynomials have constant term $1$. The right side is an algebraic unit. A rational algebraic unit must be $1$ or $-1$, whereas $(Y+1)/Y>1$. This contradiction proves the claim. Kesten's theorem now implies that $D(R)$ is unbounded.

As $B$ ranges through large real values, $R(B)$ attains every sufficiently large integer: take $B=y(p_b+V(R-1))$. The bounded residual in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} cannot cancel an unbounded discrepancy. Hence
\[
 N(B)=\kappa\log\log B-2\kappa\log\log\log B+O(1)
\]
is false as an all-height assertion. The conclusion is about the claimed smooth remainder, not a failure of the leading law or of the precise height inverse.

\subsection{The exact unresolved criterion}
For this fixed rotation, the following statements are equivalent:
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:equiv}
 N(B)&=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+o(\log t)
       &&\text{as all real }B\to\infty,\quad t=\log\log B;\notag\\
 D(R)&=o(\log R)&&\text{as integers }R\to\infty.
\end{align}
Sufficiency follows from~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} and $R(B)\sim t/(2\alpha V)$. For necessity take $B=y(p_b+V(R-1))$. Then $R(B)=R$ exactly, and $\log t=\log R+O(1)$ by~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}. Subtracting the smooth terms in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} gives the required $o(\log R)$ estimate.

Unboundedness of $D(R)$ does not disprove this finer condition. An $O(\log R)$ bound alone would also be insufficient to identify the second coefficient, since its error has exactly that scale. Equidistribution supplies only $o(R)$, and the effective power saving~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:powersaving} is still too large. No $o(\log R)$ estimate is claimed.

\section{Comparison and further research}\label{fup:hc:sec:comparison}
\subsection{The scope differs from Report 25}
Report 25 classifies an entire fixed positive native fiber: seventeen supplied coordinates are forced, and all remaining tuples are parametrized by two Pell indices~\cite{fup:hc:r25}. Its height law follows from exact finite floor counts, exceptional baseline terms, hyperbola decomposition and Euler summation. In its notation the leading count is logarithmic in height, with a proved unsmoothed square-root second term and a smaller power error.

Here the input, compiler, repunit and prime-scale choice are fixed, but only the prescription $m=p\psi_A(p)$ and $S=p+2m$ is counted. The free main index runs through one CRT progression, selected by an irrational-rotation window. No classification of all admissible auxiliary indices or all signed19 witnesses has been proved. The count is double-logarithmic in height, and its second-term question is governed by the explicit discrepancy~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:discrepancy}. Report 25's two-index floor-sum error cannot be imported into this problem.

Pell growth, Binet expansion and careful height inversion are standard shared tools. The specific issue resolved here is the persistence of the exact rotation discrepancy through exponentially small strict-ratio endpoint perturbations, and the consequent bounded-remainder obstruction. This comparison is a statement of mathematical scope, not a literature-wide novelty claim.

\subsection{Concrete questions left open}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The finer discrepancy scale.} Determine whether $D(R)=o(\log R)$ for the explicit fixed $\theta,\xi,I$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:rotation}. By~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:equiv}, this is exactly the missing estimate for the stated triple-logarithmic second coefficient. A result only on a subsequence or after averaging would not establish the all-height claim.
\item \textbf{Usable effective constants.} Specialize explicit logarithmic-form constants to one fixed compiler, input and scale, and bound the last possible endpoint disagreement. The present proof gives effectivity in principle, without a practical numerical exponent or onset. It does not materialize the enormous supplied tuples.
\item \textbf{Larger families.} Characterize other permitted auxiliary choices at a fixed main index, then count their union without duplication. The current canonical prescription offers a lower-bound subfamily inside the full fiber, not an upper bound or an exhaustive parametrization.
\item \textbf{Dependence on the scale.} Study how density and height constants vary over prime-scale choices, with genuinely uniform error bounds if possible. Summing fixed-scale asymptotics without such control is not justified by this report.
\end{enumerate}
The valid positive-index parent retains its positivity hypothesis. The separate raw29 and positive21 restoration questions remain outside the present theorem. No new universal operation count, degree bound, full numerical witness or machine-specific acceptance claim follows.

\section{Transfer to asymmetric signed19 orientations}\label{fup:hc:sec:transfer}
A separate pinned source comparison~\cite{fup:hc:transfer} extends the applicability of this fixed family. At ProveIt commit
\begin{writenote}
The pinned sources of this appendix are research-tree files shipped inside archive~05 as byte copies and not shipped here: \rw{complete74_asymmetric_scale_transfer.md} and \rw{.json}, \rw{complete74_equation_orientation_census.md} and \rw{.json} (the latter 1,508,808 bytes), and \rw{review_complete74_equation_orientation_census.md}, all in the research tree's \rw{native-stream-queue/} directory and equal to the current files. The transfer proof itself is shipped as \rw{05-signed19-heights-evidence-TRANSFER-PROOF.md}. Report~34 has not been reviewed by the programme.
\end{writenote}
\begin{center}\small\code{9fa99be93fb479294d5d5a2458e59e5fba8444b6},\end{center}
the asymmetric scale replaces $X=wq^3$ by $X=wq$, retaining $Y=sq^3$. The supplied $w$ has no other consumer. Hence the coordinate map
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:transfer}
 \Phi:\quad w_{\mathrm{new}}=q^2w_{\mathrm{old}}
\end{equation}
preserves $X$ and every subsequent computed quantity, including $R$, while leaving all other supplied coordinates unchanged.

There are four relevant equation orientations at each scale. Define
\[
 T_2=(ic^2)^2,\qquad K=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 U_0=jc-R,\qquad U_1=of-c.
\]
Each orientation retains \emph{both} protecting comparisons $T_2=K$ and $U_0=U_1$, and selects its auxiliary norm from
\[
 C_e(U_v^2-y^2)=1-y^2,\qquad C_0=T_2,\quad C_1=K,
 \qquad(e,v)\in\{0,1\}^2.
\]
For each parent define the corresponding child by substituting $r=R=k-hXY-1$ everywhere and deleting only the resulting identical index comparison. These are mathematically defined descendants, not a claim that every eliminated packet has been published upstream.

For each \emph{matching} orientation, the scale map~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:transfer} preserves every residual, so
\[
 G_{(1,3)}^{(e,v)}(\Phi(\mathbf w))
       =G_{(3,3)}^{(e,v)}(\mathbf w)
\]
is an all-value polynomial identity over any commutative ring. Across \emph{different} orientations, the retained protecting comparisons give equality of comparison-zero loci over every commutative ring, and hence equality of sum-of-squares zero loci over the reals. This is not an assertion that the differently oriented sum-of-squares polynomials agree away from their zero sets.

The Report 33 tuples therefore give positive supplied-witness zeros with $R=-p$ in all four symmetric orientations and, after $\Phi$, all four asymmetric orientations. In the family of this report, $q$ and $w$ are fixed. The map changes just one fixed coordinate to another fixed value, while leaving $y(p)$ and every index-selection test unchanged. After increasing the finite height threshold if necessary, the maximum is still exactly $y$. All the canonical-family height and counting conclusions of Report 34 consequently transfer with the same constants and discrepancy, up to the finite-tail convention.

This uses only the forward map. It asserts neither an integral inverse on every asymmetric-child zero nor a classification of that child's full fiber. The valid twenty-coordinate parents still supply a positive $r$ and retain their nine comparisons. No conclusion for raw29, positive21, normalized87 or coupled88 is imported. No parent degree or operation ledger is assigned to these children.


\section{Evidence and the portable release}\label{fup:hc:sec:evidence}
The construction source is pinned to ProveIt commit
\begin{center}\small\code{2dde7850ffeb9c923a7bd92e3f2ee2b5af0e36ff}.\end{center}
The report was prepared from the frozen construction, its independent audit, and the separately audited analytic proof packet. The original reviewed analytic proof, before path normalization in the packaged copy, has SHA256 identity
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\code{ff9d116c90b058be082c2c9d6ee051cc}\\
\code{f537d80821c14d10b28aec9a96d5a533}.
\end{center}
The two lines concatenate to the complete digest. The whole-packet audit reports a mathematical pass for that exact version, including the effective error and the fixed-phase bounded-remainder application. \code{PROVENANCE.json} maps this original identity to the packaged digest and records every path-only portability normalization of the accompanying analytic notes.

The evidence has three distinct roles:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The inherited construction and its audit establish full child membership, positive supplied coordinates, and the fixed arithmetic progression.
\item The analytic proof and independent height, rotation and whole-packet checks establish the asymptotic formulas and external-theorem applications.
\item The local release verifier checks file identities; the offline build script rebuilds the PDF from the supplied LaTeX. These are packaging checks, not finite tests of the unbounded mathematical assertions.
\end{enumerate}
No executable mathematical checker is included or needed for this pure analytic follow-on. No upstream code, source arithmetic schedule or historical finite checker was run to establish its claims. The source component checks are analytic notes. No third-party paper PDF is redistributed in the archive; the bibliography links to the research sources. The report is a new release and does not alter Report 33.
